LLADA-8B-INSTRUCT · ONE L40S · INDEPENDENT ADAPTER

TSP5 LoRA experiment

Status: complete · phase complete · update 6250/6250

100,000 training examples; 100 fixed validation cases. One epoch, LoRA rank 16, learning rate 0.0001. Identical before/after decoding: 512 output positions, 128 steps, temperature 0.

Complete tours count every city exactly once and close the cycle. Gap is computed only for complete tours, using coordinate distances. Invalid answers have no gap. Strict validity also requires the requested bracketed format and start at zero. These are validation results, not an untouched final test.

Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [660, 15], neighbors: [4: 443.2, 3: 673.4];
Node 1, coordinates: [86, 528], neighbors: [3: 113.8, 4: 374.3];
Node 2, coordinates: [576, 736], neighbors: [4: 365.5, 1: 532.3];
Node 3, coordinates: [122, 420], neighbors: [1: 113.8, 4: 315.8];
Node 4, coordinates: [437, 398], neighbors: [3: 315.8, 2: 365.5];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the nearest neighbor algorithm. Here, we'll outline the steps and provide the final route and total distance.

1. Start at city 0.
2. Visit the nearest city to city 0, which is city 4.
3. Visit the nearest city to city 4, which is city 1.
4. Visit the nearest city to city 1, which is city 3.
5. Visit the nearest city to city 3, which is city 2.
6. Return to city 0.

The route is: [0, 4, 1, 3, 2, 0].

To calculate the total distance, we sum to distances between the consecutive cities in
the route.

Total distance = 443.2 + 374.3 + 113.8 + 315.8 + 365.5 = 1612.6

To three route, the total distance is 1612.6.

Here's the Python code to calculate the total distance:

```python
# Define the distances
distances = {
    0: 443.2,
    3: 673.4,
    1: 374.3,
    2: 365.5,
    1: 113.8,
    4: 315.8,
    1: 113.8,
    2: 374.3,
    3: 315.8,
    4: 365.5
}

# Define the route
route = [0, 4, 1, 3, 2, 0]

# Calculate the total distance
distances = [distances[i][j] for i, j in route]
total_distance = sum(distances)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input

Node 1, coordinates: [145, 867], neighbors: [2: 557.4, 0: 690.0];
Node 2, coordinates: [621, 577], neighbors: [4: 287.6, 0: 509.0];
Node 3, coordinates: [286, 112], neighbors: [0: 80.0, 4: 323.8];
Node 4, coordinates: [551, 298], neighbors: [0: 283.6, 2: 287.6];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic search like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we can use a brute-force approach to evaluate all possible routes and find the one with the minimum distance distance.

Here's the step-by-step process:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Visit each other city once.
3. Return to city 0.
4. Calculate the total distance of the route.

Let's implement this in Python:

```python
import city_distance

def minimum_distance_tour(cities):
    route = []0]
    total_distance = 0

    for city in cities:
        if city == 0:
            route.append(city)
            total_distance += cities_distance[current][city]
            continue

        min_distance = float('inf')
        current = None

        for neighbor, distance in cities_distance[city].items():
            if distance < min_distance:
                min_distance = distance
                current = neighbor

        route.append(city)
        total_distance += cities_distance[current][city]


    return route, total_distance

cities = {
    0: [288, 192], {3: 80.0, 4: 283.6},
    1: [145, 867], {2: 557.4, 0: 690.0},
    2: [621, 577], {4: 287.6, 0: 509.0},
    3: [286, 112], {0: 80.0, 4: 323.8},
    4: [551, 298], {0: 283.6, 2: 287.6}
}

route, total_distance = minimum_distance_tour(cities)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the A* algorithm. The, I'll use the A* algorithm to find the solution.

### A* Algorithm Implementation

1. **Define**:
   - `f(n)`: The total cost from the start node to the current node `n`.
   - `path`: The path taken to reach the current node `n`.
   - ` **h(n)**: The cost from reach the node `n` to the goal node (

2. **Initialize**:
   - `f(0)`: The cost from reach start node to0 is node 0.
   - `path(0)`: The path taken to reach node 0 is `(0,)`.
   - `h(0)`: The cost from reach node 0 to the goal node (0) is 0.

3. **Algorithm**:
   - Select the node with the lowest `f(n)` value.
   - If thef(n) is the same, the node with the lowest `h(n)` value is selected.
   - Calculate the distance from the selected node to the goal node.
   - Repeat the above steps until the goal node (0) is reached.

### Execution

1. **Initialize**:
   - `f(0) = 0`, `path = (0, )`, `h(0) = 0`

2. **Algorithm**:

 - Select the node with the lowest `f(n)` value.

 - If thef(n) is the same, the node with the lowest `h(n)` value is selected.

 - Calculate the distance from the selected node to the goal node.

 - Repeat the above steps until the is0.

2. **Calculate the distance for each route**: For each route distance calculate the Euclidean distance between consecutive cities.
3. **Find the minimum distance route**: Compare the total distance of each route.

- Distance from city 3 to city 4: sqrt((732-763)^2 + (680-103)^2) = 577.8
- Distance from city 4 to city 0: (from city 0 to city 2) = 374.0

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm algorithm like the A* (A-star) algorithm. The A* algorithm is a popular choice for finding the shortest path in a graph, and it can a modified to find the shortest path in a tour..

Here, the step-by-step solution to find the minimum* closed tour:

1. Initialize the starting city as city 0.
2. Create a priority to store the cities to visit.
3. While the priority is not empty:
   a. Get the city from the priority with the lowest distance.
   b. If the city is visited, skip it.
  .. If the city as not visited, mark it as visited and add it to the route.
   d. For each neighbor of the current city, calculate the distance and add it to the priority.
4. Repeat step-3 until all cities been visited.
5. Return to the starting city.

Let's apply these steps to find the minimum-distance closed tour:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Visit city 1.
3. Visit city 4.
4. Visit city 2.
5. Visit city 3.
6. Return to city 0.

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic search algorithm like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we can use a brute-force approach to find the possible routes and calculate their total distances.

Here's the step-by-step process:

1. Calculate at city 0 and find the nearest neighbor.
2.
3. Calculate the distance neighbor to the next city.
4. Repeat the process until all cities are visited.
5. Return to city 0 to complete the tour.

Let's implement the solution:

Code:

```python
import math

def min_t_distance(cities):
    route = [0]
    total_distance = 0
    
    # i the range of cities
    for i in range(1ities):
        min_distance = float('inf')
        for neighbor = neighbor
            distance = math.sqrt((neighbor.x - route[0])**2 + (neighbor - route[1])**2)
            if distance < min_distance:
                min_distance = distance
                neighbor = neighbor
        
        route.append(neighbor)
        total_distance += distance
    
    # Return to city
0
    route.append(0)
    total_distance += route[-1] + route[0]
    
    return route, total_distance

cities = {
    "Node 0": {3: 147.4, 4: 412.5},
    "Node 1": {2: 235.1, 0: 706.7},
    "Node 2": {1: 235.1, 0: 717.0},
    "Node 3": {0: 147.4, 4: 322.5},
    "Node 4": {3: 322.5, 0: 412.5}
}

route, total_distance = min_t_distance(cities)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.3f}")
```

Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. ### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [601, 209], neighbors: [4: 419.5, 3: 517.4];
Node 1, coordinates: [731, 750], neighbors: [3: 39.2, 4: 175.8];
Node 2, coordinates: [227, 930], neighbors: [1: 535.2, 3: 536.8];
Node 3, coordinates: [718, 713], neighbors: [1: 39.2, 4: 147.2];
Node 4, coordinates: [789, 584], neighbors: [3: 147.2, 1: 175.8];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we can use a brute-force approach to find the optimal route.

Here's-force approach solution:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Move to city 1 (distance 535.2).
3. Move to city 1 (distance 39.2).
4. Move to city 3 (distance 536.8).
5. Move to city 4 (distance 147.2).
6. Return to city 0 (distance 419.5).

The total distance of this route is:

39.2 + 535.2 + 536.8 + 147.2 + 419.5 = 1638.9

However, the route is a tour, not a closed tour. To make a closed tour, we need to return to city 0.

So, the optimal closed tour is:

Route: [0  1  1  3  4  0], Objective: 1638.00

Note: The brute-force approach may not be the most efficient solution, especially for larger cities. A more efficient algorithm, like the A* algorithm, can be used to find the optimal route.

Code in Python:

```python
import math

def euclidean_distance(point1, point2):
    return math.sqrt((point1[0] - point2[0]) ** 2 + (point1[1] - point2[1]) ** 2)

def calculate_distance(route):
    total_distance = 0
    for i in range(len(route) - 1):
        total_distance += euclidean_distance(route[i], route[i + 1])

    total_distance

route = [0, 1, 1, 3, 4, 0]
total_distance = calculate_distance(route)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

Output:
```
Route: [0, 1, 1, 3, 4, 0],: 1638.00
```

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use the Traveling Neighbor algorithm. Here are the steps:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Move to the nearest city that is not visited.
3. Repeat until all cities are visited.
4. Return to city 0.

1. Start at city 0.
2. Move to city 3 (nearest city city 0).
3. Move to city 1 (nearest to city 3).
4. Move to city 2 (nearest to city 1).
5. Move to city 4 (nearest to city 2).
6. Return to city 0 (nearest to city 4).

The route is: 0 -> 3 -> 1 -> 2 -> 4 -> 0.

- Distance from 0 to 3: 376.4
- Distance from 3 to 1: 407.2
- Distance from 1 to 2: 504.5
- Distance from 2 to 4: 420.8
- Distance from 4 to 0: 760.9

    4: 420.8

Prompt

### Instruction
Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [113, 699], neighbors: [2: 169.1, 4: 623.6];
Node 1, coordinates: [844, 331], neighbors: [3: 579.5, 4: 586.3];
Node 2, coordinates: [35, 849], neighbors: [0: 169.1, 4: 790.8];
Node 3, coordinates: [977, 895], neighbors: [1: 579.5, 0: 886.0];
Node 4, coordinates: [303, 105], neighbors: [1: 586.3, 0: 623.6];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic search algorithm. as an AI language model, I will use the A* algorithm to solve the problem.

Here is the Python code:



```
import math
from heapq import heappush, heappop

def euclidean(p1, p2):
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2)

def a_star(cities):
    queue = [(0, 0)]
    visited = {0   
    distance = 0

    while queue:
        current, cost = heappop(queue)
        # if we have visited all cities
        if len(visited) == 5:
            return

        route = [current]
        total_distance = 0

        for node, distance in cities[current].items():
            if node not in visited:
                visited.add(node)
                route.append(node)
                total_distance += distance
                heushush(queue, (node, cost + distance))

    return route, total_distance

cities = {
    0: {2: 169.1, 4: 623.6},
    1: {3: 579.5, 4: 586.3},
    2: {0: 169.1, 4: 790.8},
    3: {1: 579.5, 0: 886.0},
    4: {1: 586.3, 0: 623.6}
}

route, total_distance = a_star(cities)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

- 0.3 = 50.5

- ((1,2)) = sqrt(116^2 + 116^2) = 154.8
- ((1,2)) = sqrt(116^2 + 116^2) = 154.8

Prompt

### Instruction
Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [3, 116], neighbors: [3: 141.4, 1: 597.2];
Node 1, coordinates: [256, 657], neighbors: [3: 468.0, 4: 470.5];
Node 2, coordinates: [775, 926], neighbors: [4: 138.2, 1: 584.6];
Node 3, coordinates: [105, 214], neighbors: [0: 141.4, 1: 468.0];
Node 4, coordinates: [637, 933], neighbors: [2: 138.2, 1: 470.5];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we can use a brute-force approach to explore all possible routes and find the one with the minimum distance distance.

Here's the step-by-step process:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Visit each other city once.
3. Return to city 0.

### Calculate the Euclidean distance between each city

- City 0 to City 1: 597.2
- City 0 to City 3: 141.4
- City 0 to City 4: 470.5
- City 1 to City 3: 468.0
- City 1 to City 4: 470.5
- City 2 to City 1: 584.6
- City 2 to City 4: 138.2
- City 3 to City 0: 141.4
- City 3 to City 1: 468.0
- City 4 to City 2: 138.2
- City 4 to City 1: 470.5

### Solution using brute-force

We brute-force brute-force all possible routes and calculate the total distance to find the minimum distance.

Here's the Python code for the brute-force approach:

```python
import math

def euclidean_distance(point1, point2):
    return math.sqrt((point1[0] - point2[0]) ** 2 + (point1[1] - point2[1]) ** 2)

def calculate_distance(route):
    distance = 0
    for i in range(len(route) - 1):
        distance += euclidean_distance(route[i], route[i + 1])
    return distance

route = [0, 1, 3, 2, 4, 0]
total_distance = calculate_distance(route)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.3f}")
```

Output:
```
Route: [0, 1, 3, 2, 4, 0], Objective: 1770.00
```

7. Update the search space with the next city and the costs.
8. Repeat the 3 to 7, and repeat the process until the goal the is reached.

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use the Traveling Sales algorithm (NeTSP) which is a heuristic algorithm for the Traveling Sales Salesman Problem (TSP). Here's the step-by-step solution:

1. **Initialize the Start Point**: Start at city 0.
2. **Visit the Nearest Neighbor**: Move to city nearest neighbor city 1 (distance 652.7).
3. **Visit the Nearest Neighbor**: Move to city nearest neighbor city 2 (distance 122.6).
4. **Visit the Nearest Neighbor**: Move to city nearest neighbor city 4 (distance 484.6).
5. **Visit the Nearest Neighbor**: Move to city nearest neighbor city 3 (distance 180.7).
6. **Visit the Nearest Neighbor**: Return to city nearest neighbor city 0 (distance 669.0).

However, since we need to return at city 0, we should to from city 3 to city 0 (distance 669.0) instead the the last step to go from city 6 to city 0.

Now, let's calculate the total distance:

1. From city 0 to city 1: 652.7.
2. From city 1 to city 2: 122.6.
3. From city 2 to city 4: 484.6.
4. From city 4 to city 3: 180.7.
5. From city 3 to city 0: 669.0.

```

```
